On the other hand, when it comes to the valuation or pricing of benefits, it is a completely separate project. Of course, if someone is planning an investment, they are counting on this rather than other revenues and are also looking at the benefits tariffs. He is a graduate of the history of the Jagiellonian University. In 2011 he received a PhD in humanities in history at the Pontifical University of John Paul II in Cracow. He was one of the closest associates to sleep. Professor Janusz Kurtyki. The 20th century. This meant that our previous assumptions had to be reviewed. The new Management Board also submitted its business plan, which already covers these larger assumptions. Currently, the entities to be involved in the restructuring programme are Węglokoks SA, as well as Enea's energy company, which notified the access earlier last year. As a larger pool of measures will be needed to engage in this restructuring process, these two entities are taken seriously into account. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u162	I ask you, Minister Piotr, to give me an answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u163	Dear Marshal! Dear Mr Fellowship, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! However, in the context of this question at the beginning, I would like to say two sentences about a certain systemic remark. It should be stressed that spending European funds, in particular in the healthcare sector, should not only focus on the rate of spending, but above all on the validity and quality of investment, while not generating additional costs for the public health system. Investment measures in the health sector generate an increase in public expenditure from the National Health Fund for the provision of health services. Any investment that increases the scope of benefits makes it necessary to increase the spending of the National Health Fund to finance their possibility in the public health system. Investment from European sources should therefore be planned in such a way as to increase quality and accessibility, but at the same time not to generate unnecessary costs in the public health system. In this context, it is worth recalling the findings of the 2013 NIK audit. NIK inspection, report on this inspection. The purchase and use of medical equipment co-financed by regional operational programmes, May 2013. In the overall assessment of the controlled activity, NIK carried out the following important assessment, and I quote: In the framework of the Regional Operational Programmes for 2007-2013, health care establishments could apply for EU funds to finance projects for the purchase of modern medical equipment adapting their equipment to existing standards and allowing for broadening the scope of health services provided. And further: However, more than 30% of the 33 hospitals audited did not achieve the planned results, in particular the expected number of benefits, while implementing the assumptions adopted at the level of 3 to 90%. In the NIK's assessment, the degree of use of the apparatus examined had an impact on the decision to grant the grant without an analysis of the health needs and to assess the availability of the benefits provided using such equipment, as well as the failure to ensure adequate coordination of its purchasing activities. This situation was also affected by irregularities found in hospitals, not ensuring adequate conditions for the provision of benefits as well as delays in the preparation of the diagnostic room. As a result, due to the low value of contracts concluded with, or even lack of, the National Health Fund, the possibility of using the equipment purchased to provide paid health benefits has been undermined by the financial results of certain controlled entities. The need to concentrate on the validity, quality of investment in the health sector has also been recognised by the European Commission, which is reflected in the Partnership Agreement, as well as in the requirements set for the health sector in ex ante conditionality. Therefore, as well as the results of the NIK audit and the expectations of the European Commission at the Ministry of Health, measures have been taken to coordinate investments made from European funds at both regional and central level. To this end, in line with the Partnership Agreement, a Steering Committee for the coordination of interventions in the health sector has been established, health needs maps containing detailed information on health infrastructure have been developed. In addition, the Minister of Health has developed a tool to assess the purpose of the investment, i.e. IOWISZ's health investment application assessment form, through which the competent institutions will be able to carry out a uniform analysis of the projects planned for implementation. Currently, the Seym is being revised by the Act on Public-funded healthcare provision, which introduces this tool. At the same time, it should be stressed that the Ministry of Development itself coordinates regional operational programmes. One of the tools for coordination is this steering committee as a sub-committee of the Partnership Agreement. This steering committee for coordination is composed of representatives of regions, central offices, including the Ministry of Health, Ministry of Development, Ministry of Finance, and representatives of the so-called. the third sector, i.e. non-public entities and a number of associations. However, its powers and tasks are limited, mainly to the adoption of the so-called. action plans for the health sector containing timetables for competitions and proposals for non-competition projects, issuing recommendations for selection criteria. These tasks shall be carried out on an ongoing basis and, in particular, the Committee shall not have the power to impose time limits and detailed conditions for launching the selection of projects on the various marshals. These are the competences of individual managing authorities, i.e. marshals, and monitoring committees for each operational programme. As regards the transparency of the selection of the projects referred to by the honourable Member, it should be stressed that the rules in force within the framework of the European Funds allow the use of the non-competition procedure. The draft budget for 2017 is also underway. The project will also present the financial plan of the Social Insurance Fund, which will set out the revenue and expenditure plan, including the financing of the shortage of this fund in the following year. Information on the financial situation of the Social Insurance Fund is widely available, as this year another long-term forecast by 2060 of the pension fund has been drawn up by the Social Insurance Institution in three options for shaping the economic situation of the country. The balance of the fund is negative throughout the forecast period, which means that the statutory solvency guarantee for social security benefits will have to be implemented in the long term. It should be recalled that the Social Insurance Act has identified various sources of funding for the Social Insurance Fund shortage, such as a grant from the state budget, interest-free loans from the state budget, possibly bank loans received with the agreement of the Minister responsible for public finances. The FUS pension fund may also be provided with funds from the Demographic Reserve Fund to supplement the deficit due to demographic reasons or in the form of an interest-free return loan. With regard to determining the consequences of the proposed changes on the financial situation of the Social Insurance Fund, any estimate in the horizon by 2025 is presented in the draft position of the Government. I will move on to these estimates further. It should only be noted that in the adopted 19 July this year. The position of the Government requests that the Sejm consider the possibility of supplementing the presidential bill on modifications of certain solutions and that the Act enter into force at the earliest on 1 October 2017 due to the need to carry out all activities preparing the ZUS and the KRUS to carry out statutory amendments. Taking into account the government's request will affect the effects presented, but it will be possible to estimate them once new forecasts of the Social Security Office have been received, taking into account the new date of entry into force of the Act. With regard to the sources of funding for the reduction of the retirement age, it should be stressed that the government is pursuing fiscal policies using the instruments available, implementing its economic agenda in a way that promotes economic growth, while taking into account the constraints under national and EU law. When deciding on the final arrangements for determining the retirement age and the deadline for introducing the measures adopted, the government takes into account the possibilities of public finances in terms of financing the costs of reducing the retirement age. At this point it should be noted that since 2015 the budget law has been drawn up taking into account a stabilising expenditure rule aimed at ensuring the sustainability of Polish public finances. At present, attention in the budgetary process is focused on how a specific amount of expenditure is allocated in advance, not at its level. Actions on the expenditure side carried out within the framework provided by the so-called "government." stabilizing the expenditure rule is accompanied by measures to rebuild the stream of tax revenue, inter alia. by sealing the tax system. The government is carrying out intensified efforts to seal the tax system, which is consistent with the Ecofin Council's 2014 recommendation to Poland. The reform of the tax administration and its provision with modern IT tools will reduce the scope of VAT fraud and increase the collection of other taxes. The closing of the tax gap is currently a priority because the problems that have persisted in this respect for several years have led to the need to consolidate public finances that are too much on the expenditure side. As a result of the sealing of the tax system, it will be possible, in a way that is safe for the state's finances, to achieve social policy objectives, thus improving the economic situation of the household sector. At the same time, I would like to point out that the annex to the 2016 update of the convergence programme presents the measures sealing the tax system in the years 2017-2019. Most of the actions presented there are at an advanced stage of legislative work or have entered into force. With regard to the question concerning the fulfilment of the disposal provided for in Article. The 50th Public Finance Act, which requires the determination of the financial consequences, the indication of the source of funding, the description of the objectives of the new tasks and measures setting out the degree of achievement of the objectives, should indicate that, on the basis of the above-mentioned provision of the Public Finance Act, the Council of Ministers is required to present in the explanatory memorandum the above-mentioned provisions. information only for its own legal acts, i.e. projects adopted by the Council of Ministers. In view of the presidential proposal, the Council of Ministers is not obliged to do so. However, the government's position towards the presidential bill adopted by the Council of Ministers on 19 July presented the financial implications for the Social Insurance Fund, including the ESA 2010 principles applied by the European Commission, as well as the financial consequences in the farmers' social security system. So that I can present these effects if there is such expectation. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u257	Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. I'm sorry about the tooling, but my throat is professionally ripped off, and I still have to... Allow me to present to you a report on the activities of the Supreme Court for 2015. The Supreme Court is a special judicial authority appointed to exercise justice by ensuring the legality and uniformity of the case law of the general courts and of the military. Such judicial supervision shall be carried out through the recognition of cassation and other remedies, as well as the adoption of resolutions determining legal issues. It is also a matter of political functions of the Supreme Court to recognise electoral protests and to determine the validity of elections to the Sejm and Senate, as well as the election of the President, the validity of the national referendum and the constitutional referendum. I stress this this year, especially in 2015, because almost all these elections took place and they delayed the examination of cases by the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs, because it is up to this House to deal with these issues. In addition, the Supreme Court gives its opinion on the draft laws and other normative acts on the basis of which the courts rule and function, but also on the laws for which the Supreme Court considers it appropriate. Each of these areas of activity of the Supreme Court poses different challenges and provides a separate perspective for assessing its activities. The primary task of the Supreme Court is to perform the judicial function. It's obvious. In this context, there was a slight increase in the number of cases in 2015, but it is not shockingly large. In total, 11 214 cases were brought to the Supreme Court. Cassation complaints and cassations prevaild - 7971, the most of which, because 3662, they were brought to the Civil Chamber - this is also the largest chamber of the Supreme Court - and to the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs - 2519 and also to the Criminal Chamber - 1778. To the Military Chamber, too, but only 12. A total of 173 legal issues, mostly in the Civil Chamber, have also been brought together and 1057 complaints have been received. The other cases consisted of appeals against military decisions of district courts, actions for annulment of final decisions, competition and consumer cases, actions for the length of proceedings, actions for reopening, requests for referral to another court, cases for pardon and incidental applications, as well as disciplinary actions. In this last group of cases, i.e. in the group of disciplinary matters, the Supreme Court ruled on disciplinary liability of judges - 82 cases, as well as other professional groups: lawyers, lawyers, notaries, prosecutors - only 76. Now, in addition to these figures after the committee, I would like to say that in matters concerning disciplinary matters, the judgment of first instance was maintained or amended in 31 cases, nine cases were repealed and re-examined and one was suspended. There were immunity cases and some other procedural matters. The interest of the ladies and gentlemen of the committee has aroused the penalties that have been imposed in disciplinary proceedings. So there was only one removal from the occupied position. There were nine reprimands, there were reprimands, a transfer to another office - five, a submission of a judge from office - one, as I said, and a suspension of the increase in salary for a period of 3 years - two. In total, 24 such punishments were imposed. The judge was found guilty and the penalty procedure was dismissed - there were five such cases, two of which were removed from the penalty. So it was a small matter. In total, the Supreme Court recognised 10 502 cases, 7554 actions, i.e. not all cases were identified, 1022 complaints and 154 legal issues. In the information provided to ladies and gentlemen, there is no assessment of the percentage of cassation complaints that have been accepted for consideration. I told the committee it was 30%. I've examined it in the meantime, and it's actually about 30%, i.e. There is 25% in the Civilian Chamber and 39.2 in the Civilian Chamber. In the Penalty Chamber, they're almost all going through it. Overall, these legal issues were very much because they are the most difficult. By moving to the organisation of the Supreme Court, in my first approach, I would like to say that when it comes to determining the positions of judges in the Supreme Court and the number of governors, the number of judges has been increased and the number of governors has not changed, because the organisation of the court has not changed. This figure was defined as 93 persons and the cast as at 31 December 2015 was only 84 persons. This is due to the fact that some judges have retired without prior notice, although this is not a large number, but also from the procedure of recruitment for the position of Supreme Court Judge. This is currently happening and we are waiting for the President to swear four judges. The Supreme Court used the assistance of nine judges sent by the Minister of Justice at the request of the first President of the Supreme Court to rule in the Civil Chamber - one person, in the Chamber of Work - one person, in the Criminal Chamber - seven persons. The Penalty Chamber is in a rather convenient situation here, as judges of the Military Chamber have also performed judicial duties throughout the year in the Penalty Chamber. As regards the composition of the Supreme Court judges, most of them are appointed from common courts, i.e. the culmination of the judicial career - 60 persons, as well as military courts - eight people. The scientific community - only 14 people, lawyers - only one person. He may wonder whether it's a matter of pay or not that lawyers don't come to the Supreme Court, in any case very prominent ones. The case-law of each Chamber concerned a very diverse and complex group of issues. This was mainly the result of significant regulatory instability of the system, numerous amendments and bad inter-temporal rules. The interpretation resolutions of the Supreme Court were therefore of particular importance. In addition to the harmonisation of the views expressed in them, the practice of law enforcement, lawyers and lawyers has also been shaped by the practice of law enforcement. In the final result, the resolution of the Supreme Court also translates into the stability of the law and, I hope, the deepening of citizens' confidence in the state. The increasing complexity and interdisciplinaryness of legal problems caused the Supreme Court to refer repeatedly to international law, European law, the case law of the ECHR and the Court of Justice of the European Union. I would like, if your marshal would allow us, to move on to the issues raised in the various chambers. Very briefly. What I think is most important for you to know. The Civil Chamber is the largest chamber with very difficult, complex laws and problems. There is a clear advantage here in the broad-based process issue. This procedure is changing all the time. Therefore, this legal principle may bind the courts even in a slightly changed legal state. These relations with administrative law are very large, which is precisely related to compensation related to the Decree on ownership and use of land in the area of m.st. Warsaw. These are resolutions III CZP 88/15 and V CSK 5/15. It takes the view that, in cassation proceedings - it is probably still valid - the repeal of the contested decision is only possible in the part in which it did not contain a decision on a criminal measure which was mandatory. In the end, the disputed and non-abbreviated interpretation problem concerning the scope of the ruling by the Court of Cassation was resolved in this way. A particularly important issue from the point of view of the defendant's procedural interests was the resolution of the Supreme Court I KZP 4/15, which stated that Article. 377 k.p.k. is a stand-alone basis for the hearing which was adjourned, still when the defendant, who had not given an explanation and had been informed of the earlier date of the hearing, was notified of the new time limit in any way provided for in the law and did not appear for the hearing. Among the procedural aspects of the resolution of the Criminal Chamber, which will still be important, it is worth noting the issue of the admissibility of polygraphs - polygraphs. As a result of the deliberations carried out, the Supreme Court concluded that it was unacceptable to use the polygraph - in any way - during the proceedings of the hearing. The resolution on the use of the polygraph will have meaning for other sections of the law, including labour law, where polygraphs are often used. A resolution on the running of games on slots was important in the activities of the Criminal Chamber, but the matter was not settled in substance, but was suspended until the European Union settled the case. Many of the resolutions of the Criminal Chamber concerned the provisions of the special section of the Criminal Code. The legal problem that is relevant to practice was resolved in the resolution of I KZP 5/15 on money laundering. It was found that the subject of a money laundering crime could be held in a bank account with cash up to an amount equal to the value of the property benefits affected by the enforcement operation. In turn, the resolution I KZP 7/15 states that the deliberate imprisonment of another person may be regarded as a crime against humanity, the criminality of which is not subject - I will remind - to the statute of limitations, even if it does not carry out the marks of the prohibited act referred to in Article. 118a § Point 2 k.k.a. Among the judgments issued outside the legislative procedure, i.e. outside the resolutions, special attention should be paid to the issues raised in connection with the entry into force of the two amendments to K.p.k., but I think that this will already be less important, I will not refer to it. On the other hand, from the perspective of the practice of law enforcement, it is important to have a judgment stating that the marks of a prohibited act referred to in Article. 302 also exhausts those who are insolvent, cannot satisfy all creditors, but is also punished if one creditor does not satisfy one. The Supreme Court also found - and this is very important practically - that the final decision of the court to maintain the order of the prosecutor to terminate the investigation in the in-rem phase is a judgment concluding the investigation, which opens the way to appeal against the court's order with an emergency cassation pursuant to Article. 521 §1 k.p.k. I'm moving on to the Chamber of Labour, Social Security and Public Affairs, which is closest to me, as I always say. The issue of social security, pensions and pensions prevaild here, and I hope, having listened to the debate on the new pension law, that inter-temporal issues would be very carefully identified. First of all, it is important to draw attention to the resolution of the enlarged composition of the judges, which was granted, by way of exception, the power of the legal principle. It stated that the right to be subject to social insurance by a member of the board of directors of a public limited liability company who entered into a management service contract with that company in the course of his own business is not that economic activity but that of a service contract. This has been the subject of the Chamber's ruling for years, but there have been different rulings, and this position has dominated it. The second issue, which is important for the practice of applying social security law, was the issue of farmers' social security issues. No individual instruction by the pension authority on the obligation under Article. 5a The Act on Social Insurance for Farmers, which is an obligation to submit a certificate on the amount of income tax on income from non-agricultural economic activities, i.e. the link between economic and agricultural activities, does not result in the repeal of the Article. Article 5a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 6 effects in the form of the cessation of social security for farmers. This is very important for farmers, they remain in this system, which is of course more convenient. With regard to labour law issues, it is worth paying attention to the decision III PZP 5/15, which concerned the reimbursement of travel expenses for delegated judges. The Supreme Court considered that the rate of 30 gr per km was sufficient, as requested by the legislator. It was very important to resolve doubts about the Group Exemption Act for an employer employing at least 20 employees and making statements changing as to whether the number of statements changing includes the number of persons released as a group exemption, which requires a specific procedure. The Supreme Court considered that it was necessary to ask the Court of Justice of the European Union for a preliminary ruling on the interpretation of the concept of collective exemption as to whether it was definitive or amending, and therefore ordered the General Court to do so at second instance. I would like to point out that I have already signalled that the presidential elections, parliamentary elections, national referendums and supplementary elections to the Senate have led to a significantly excessive involvement of the House in the public hearing, and that is why these arrears are greater than every other year in this Chamber, because these matters need to be resolved in a very timely manner. As far as the organizational activities of the Supreme Court are concerned, it is primarily the dissemination of information on the case law of the Supreme Court. We have a website, continued to publish under the new titles of the series "Studies and Supreme Court Analysis." Science materials, as well as studies and analysis of the Supreme Court. Review of the Court of First Instance. A collection of Supreme Court decisions was published - the Disciplinar Court. It was also important that the Supreme Court press officer, who monitored the media and, in the event of some confusion, ambiguity, responded very quickly to all these questions. As in previous years, the Supreme Court initiated numerous scientific conferences in 2015. There were very important conferences on appeal, cassation and renewal proceedings in the new model of the criminal trial. The European Public Prosecutor's Office has been an international scientific conference on relations between the European Public Prosecutor's Office and national authorities. In addition, the first Supreme Court President and judges participated in numerous conferences - maybe I won't be taking your time, exchanging all these conferences. Several delegations also attended the invitation of the first president with official visits. In connection with the parliamentary elections in the Supreme Court there was also a representative of the OSCE, who received a very thorough analysis of our electoral code and the situation. All this was happening in the organizational sphere as in previous years. I can therefore say that the conditions under which the Supreme Court operated during the reporting period, i.e. in 2015, were not substantially different from those in previous years. The objectives of the Supreme Court have not changed, there have been no more significant modifications in individual chambers, in the functioning of these chambers. There has been a greater impact of cases, but this is not a very large impact and it can generally be said that in civil chambers and work the expectation of a resolution is 10 months, in the Military Chamber - 2 months, in the Criminal Chamber it is 10 months, in total in these three it is 10 months. We believe that this is in line with European standards. There is generally no chronicity of proceedings in the Supreme Court. There is a large increase in the impact of legal issues, which of course requires increased intellectual activity also of the Office of Studies and Analysis, but I can generally say that the questions addressed to the Supreme Court are formulated better than in previous years, and this is very good news. They are generally valid. It is very rare to say that we refuse to answer. The practice of the Supreme Court has continued for many years in the justifications, in particular resolutions, but also provisions on legal issues, the assessment of the whole case law and the systematicisation of this case law, the development and evolution of case law has been accepted. This is useful for courts, as well as lawyers and lawyers. The state of law is not satisfactory, but in the High House I will no longer talk about it, because it is not very pleasant for you. Code of civil procedure e.g. it really lost its readability, it's not consistent, because it's constantly being updated and it's really hard to recognize. The issues concerning the state of the legislation have been presented by me in this little book of notes about the irregularities and gaps found in the law, which we formulate on the basis of the case law of the Supreme Court, not what I believe to be Margaret Gersdorf, but what results from the judgments. I would like to say that the results obtained, the substantive activity, the judicial activity of the Supreme Court should be assessed and it is positive by me. This also allows for an optimistic forecast of the future performance of tasks, especially as we have more administrative posts, which allows the Supreme Court to function better in administrative terms this year, 2016. Maintaining the efficiency of the Supreme Court is, of course, the personal merit of judges who ensure that citizens are given effective judicial protection. In a broader context, we must also not forget the activity of all the employees of the Supreme Court, the administrative staff, the service office, the reading room that we have created, and above all the members of the Bureau of Studies and Analysis and Assistants of Judges. Thank you very much, Marshal. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u263	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! Madam First Chairman! High Izbo! The Supreme Court is a constitutional body appointed together with common courts for the exercise of justice. This objective, in accordance with the Law of 23 November 2002, is pursued in particular by ensuring the legality and uniformity of the case-law of the General Courts and of the Military Courts by recognising the deletions and other remedies, by adopting resolutions on legal matters and by resolving other matters referred to in the laws. The examination of the report submitted and discussed by you by the first President gives all grounds for a positive assessment of the activities of the Supreme Court. It must be concluded that the proceedings are conducted in a proper manner, with respect to standards and standards, but also without chronicity. Let me just point out that today's debate concerns the assessment of the activities of the Supreme Court itself as an institution, not as a whole of the general court. As indicated in the report, there was a slightly higher impact of cases in 2015, in particular cassation and cassation complaints. However, this did not lead to an extended period of recognition. The average waiting time for a substantive investigation was in the Civil Chamber, the Labour House, Social Insurance and Public Affairs and the Criminal Chamber for up to 10 months, and in the Military Chamber for up to 2 months from the date of impact. This state fully corresponds to the standards set out in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In total, 11 214 cases were brought to the Supreme Court. By comparison, in 2014 they were received by 11 065. The Supreme Court recognised a total of 10 502 cases, which is the same result as the previous year. Among them, the vast majority were cassation complaints and cassations, which were 7554. In addition to this preliminary part of the assessment, it is also worth recalling that the increasing complexity of the legal problems examined has led the Supreme Court to refer repeatedly to international and European law, the case law of the European Court of Human Rights and the Court of Justice of the European Union. Turning to the judicial activity of the Supreme Court, it should be pointed out that its legislative activity is an essential element. It is primarily intended to ensure legal certainty and strengthen citizens' confidence in the state. First of all, High Izbo, I'm going to discuss the issues of the Civil Chamber. In 2015, the Supreme Court adopted 78 resolutions in this House. This represents a slight increase compared to the previous year, in which 74 resolutions were issued. Among them, twelve were issued in the composition of seven judges, the first President of the Supreme Court made four such requests, the Attorney General - two, the Ombudsman - one application and the Supreme Court - five applications. At this point, I would like to point out that the discussion may be about the failure to give any of the resolutions the rule of law. This is because they have the strongest influence on the certainty of the legal system. This issue was discussed at a meeting of the Committee on Justice. The explanations given by you to the first President have its basis and the activities of the Civil Chamber in this respect seem reasonable. A very positive phenomenon, worthy of emphasis and praise, is also a decrease in the number of refusals to adopt a resolution compared to the previous year. The subject of the legal issues covered by the work was very diverse. However, it should be pointed out that there is still a clear advantage here, as in previous years, of the broadly understood procedural issue. In this area, the Supreme Court distinguishes two of its resolutions, i.e. those about the sign. III CZP 6/15 and II CZP 36/15, representing the jurisdiction of the substantive and local courts, which were very important for the general court. As indicated in the report, other distinguished case files were those relating to compensation from an insurer to insured persons for the activities of insurance agents. This clearly illustrates the weakness of regulation in this regard. This should therefore constitute an alarm and a signpost for the legislator to undertake specific legislative work. The report for the activities of the Civil Chamber in 2015 distinguishes a number of important and important judgments. For the purpose of today's debate and due to certain time constraints, I would like to mention only a few of them. They are certainly to be included in the resolution of 30 January 2015, in which the Supreme Court assessed the jurisdictional grounds and the practical usefulness of the so-called procura. the total inappropriate consisting in establishing a single proxy and making the validity of his legal activities on behalf of the company subject to the will of a member of the company's management. The enlarged composition of the Supreme Court, in resolving this extremely difficult legal conflict, has taken a more formal stand and considered that the provisions of the Civil Code do not provide for the establishment of a combined procura, even though this is contrary to the fundamental principle of representation of legal persons set out in the Article. 38 Civil Code. A further resolution deserving of distinction states that the substantive validity of a judgment declaring the provision of a model contract to be inadmissible excludes an action for recognition as inadmissible provisions of the same normative content as those applied by the defendant's trader in the case in which that judgment was delivered. Even after the provision has been entered in the register, an action for recognition as an unlawful provision of the same normative content applied by a non-proposed trader shall not be excluded. This ruling is of great importance primarily for thousands of Poles deceived by companies using illegal contractual arrangements. This will certainly facilitate the enforcement of their claims before courts. When discussing the case law of the Civil Chamber, it is impossible to escape the assessment of civil proceedings. We are all aware that they differ significantly from ideal, as the first president mentioned in her speech. Their main weakness is the rules governing enforcement. Further governments have tried to make smaller or larger changes, but in general, the rules in this area introduce a lot of ambiguities and problems of interpretation. Anyone who has come into contact with enforcement proceedings, whether as a creditor or as a debtor, is aware of the existing difficulties. This claim is reflected in the achievements of the Civil Chamber. As indicated in the report, the number of enforcement issues means that, in the practice of common courts, most often in regional courts, serious interpretation difficulties are still arising from the complex and complex nature of enforcement rules. The resolution of 21 October 2015 was valid from the point of view of Poles taking up their studies in a permanent manner. III CZP 67/15. It settled the question of the limitation period for a university's public claim to pay fees by a student, so-called. tuition, indeterminate college. The Supreme Court held that a contract on the conditions of payment for non-stationary studies concluded between a public university and a student is not a contract for the provision of services to which it applies, pursuant to Article. 750 Civil Code, order regulations. Therefore, the claims from these contracts are valid on a general basis, i.e. over a longer period of 10 years. Finally, I would like to point out that it is worth emphasising, as the authors themselves stress, that the Civil Chamber continues to promote the fundamental balance of constitutional values resulting from the principles of social justice described in the article. 2 the constitution and the social market economy described in Article. 20 constitutions. As regards the activities of the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs, it should be pointed out at the outset that in 2015 there was a much larger number of cases to be dealt with. Importantly, this increase took place in all kinds of cases. There was certainly a clear increase in public affairs activity, which was a natural consequence of the 2015 elections. The presidential, parliamentary elections took place, there was a national referendum, as well as supplementary elections to the Senate. It should be clarified that the electoral code and the referendum law specify for the Supreme Court time limits for the adoption of resolutions on the validity of elections, which makes the recognition of electoral protests take place outside the order. These circumstances had a significant impact on the work of the Chamber in 2015, which also naturally affected the performance of the work. The functioning and organisation of the Chamber as a whole should therefore be assessed positively. In 2015, 35 legal issues were raised, i.e. 13 more than in the previous year. Information on the activities of the Supreme Court in 2015 raised the problem of increasing the number of cases addressed to the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs of the Supreme Court. In 2015 by 16% the number of cassation complaints has increased. During that time, she received 2.5,000. proceedings. As a result, there are also cases that the Chamber has not been able to deal with and which remain to be considered in the following year. In just two years, the number increased by 92%. In the view of the Supreme Court, one of the reasons for this situation is the too low threshold of the value of the subject matter of appeal, which makes it possible to file a cassation complaint in matters of labour law and social security. It currently stands at 10,000. PLN and is much lower than the value of other property rights cases. In that case, it's 50,000. Consideration should be given to whether it is appropriate to increase the value threshold of the subject matter of appeal in matters of labour law and social security. This would relieve the Supreme Court from cases that are not necessarily to be brought to it. The role and weight of the Supreme Court in signalling irregularities and gaps in the law is, in our opinion, crucial to the functioning of the legal system of the Republic of Poland. We've heard from a majority of the Seyms about a team of guys who defend the status quo of the previous power. It's a simple way to destroy the rule of law. In our opinion, ensuring the independence of judges is today becoming the central point of the public debate. Today I would like to remind the High Chamber of the Supreme Court's position on the Constitutional Tribunal. In its resolution, the General Assembly of the Supreme Court states that an unpublished judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal declaring non-compliance with the Constitution of a particular provision shall repeal the presumption of conformity with the Constitution upon delivery of the judgment. This should actually end discussions on this subject. The December Act governing the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal could not bind the judges of the Tribunal in a judgment on its non-constitutionality. The Supreme Court makes this clear with all its authority. We can note with appreciation that the Supreme Court can so firmly uphold the rights and independence of judges. We hope that, thanks to this primal attitude of the Supreme Court, we will avoid the threat of a kind of legal duality, i.e. a situation in which some courts recognise court rulings and some do not. The fulfilment of the statutory duties of the Supreme Court in 2015 should be assessed as positively as possible. Over 11,000. cases with diverse and complex topics, including more than 10 000. recognised, widely conducted dissemination of information about case law both through publications and the Ombudsman, numerous scientific conferences initiated by the Supreme Court, international cooperation - all of this must be appreciated today and appreciated for this work. Therefore, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of accepting information about the activities of the Supreme Court in 2015. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u283	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of two committees: the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism, I have the honour to present to the High House a report on the examination by the two committees on 19 July this year. government draft law on amending the Act on tourist services and the Act on compulsory insurance, Insurance Guarantee Fund and the Polish Office of Communication Insurances. The Commission adopted seven drafting and legislative amendments and three substantive amendments. Amendment 1 concerned Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5f. Government in Art. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5f has determined that the financial collateral provider shall verify compliance with the requirements set out in the Act and with the facts and provide the voivodship Marshal with details of the calculated amounts due to the client or information about the failure to include the reported data in whole or in part within a period of not more than 30 days from the date of receipt of the customer's notification. At that time, the provision requires that the financial collateral provider should also include a justification for why it did so. This provision shall also take into account situations where an additional investigation is necessary. The government has determined a period of not more than 60 days from the date of receipt of the notification from the customer for the conduct of this procedure. In Amendment 1, the committees propose that this time should be extended but not longer than 90 days. In the explanatory memorandum, Members stressed that if there is a large group of customers seeking reimbursement, the deadline of 60 days may be too short. Amendment 2 also concerns Article. Five, but lips. 5m, which decides that where the financial security is insufficient, the client shall immediately inform the competent Marshal of the voivodship. The provision did not specify what costs the customer may include in his information. The committee therefore proposes to clarify the provision by adding that the costs are not sufficient to cover the costs of returning customers from a tourist event or to make reimbursement of payments as defined in paragraph 1. 1 point 2, and this is about those parts of the event that either did not take place at all or did not take place in part. This provision makes the provision of the Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5 unambiguously, which will reduce the number of interpretations. Amendment 3 clarifies the guidelines for determining the contribution to the Fund. The members of both committees in this amendment propose to extend the wording of the Article. 10f paragraph. 3 of the statement clearly specifying that the amount of the contribution will depend on the place of the specific tourist event and on the type of means of transport provided in this event. Members of all parliamentary clubs have been committed to working on this project. I take the view that the climate that accompanied the work on this law stems from the awareness of the difficult situation in the tourism market, which results in a decrease in demand for travel agencies services, which results in an increase in the risk of the insolvency of travel agencies. There are many reasons for this situation, but in recent times the key cause of the crisis in tourism is the geopolitical situation and what is happening in individual countries, especially in countries often visited by Polish tourists. The government-prepared project, which is currently being launched by the High House, introduces the possibility of creating a second pillar in the system of financial security of tourism organisers. This will strengthen the overall financial security system in the event of the insolvency of the organisers of tourist events. Once again, thank you very much to Members, and I ask the High House to adopt the report of the Committee on Public Finance and to vote positively on the amendments adopted. Thank you very much.

I would like to say a few things that are important when it comes to this project. Firstly, the issue of Pillar II, why the second pillar is needed. Many people could ask whether the first pillar, which already exists and which was strengthened in the years 2012 and 2013, is not sufficient. It is believed that the second pillar is to safeguard the situation of some kind of disaster in the tourism industry, i.e. the situation in which the bankruptcy of travel agencies takes place on the basis of dominoes, i.e. the bankruptcy of one company entails further bankruptcy. Such situations occurred in 2012, but fortunately to a small extent, but due to the very difficult geopolitical situation - but not only, because such a crisis can be caused by such a crisis. volcanic eruptions, such situations can happen - we want to protect customers of travel agencies, customers of tourist entrepreneurs from the possibility of losing money, but also from the danger that they will not be able to return to the country. It should be stressed that complementarity between Pillar I and Pillar II is extremely important. And here is the question of whether this is really the case after the project has been adopted. Unfortunately, we do not ensure full complementarity because of the level of penalties provided for in the first pillar at the moment. During the committee work, we have been assured by the Minister that work will be carried out on the revision of the Tourism Services Act and that this complementarity will then be strengthened, all the more so as it is also necessary to carry out work related to the implementation of the EU Travel Services Directive. I would also like to talk about what is extremely important in this law. This law provides that this guarantee fund will operate in the so-called "warranty fund." a light model, i.e. it will be a separate account in the Insurance Guarantee Fund and it will actually be a fund that does not employ employees, it functions as a bank account. This is a good solution, but it must be taken into account that this solution has very specific consequences. One of these consequences is indeed the low cost of maintaining this fund. I will remind you of our exchange of views in the committee when we talked about the fact that this cost in the impact assessment of the regulation is set at PLN 2.5 million, and we have been assured that this PLN 2.5 million is the cost incurred only in the first year of the fund's operation, and I hope that it will be so. The costs are actually low, but the consequence of a light model fund is that we will not be able to distinguish between the insurance premiums depending on the financial standing of the entrepreneur, i.e. we do not depend on how strong the tourist company is. You can find arguments for this fund to work, and I also find these arguments in the form that costs are low, but we really need to know that these are the consequences. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u298	Thank you very much, sir. I invite Mrs Małgorzata Zwierzan, Circle of Members of Free and Solidarni.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u299	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle on the governmental draft law on amending the Act on tourist services and the Act on compulsory insurance, Insurance Guarantee Fund and the Polish Office of Communication Insurances. During each holiday season we hear about the insolvency of travel agencies and thus about the loss suffered by their customers. Often the result of insufficient protection of the interests of consumers is not only wasted leave, but also significant financial losses. The primary objective of the bill is to protect the consumer whose rights should be extended. According to the Act, the financial security in the event of the insolvency of the travel agency will be activated for all affected customers who have concluded travel events contracts during the period of validity of the financial security, regardless of the moment the trader makes a declaration of insolvency. Such assumptions of the project will make it possible for customers who, especially in the case of expensive and exotic trips, had doubts about the use of less proven but often cheaper travel agencies, will be able to buy their holidays there with peace. The threat of terrorist attacks and the wave of refugeeism in many countries has already caused and may still result in the loss of financial liquidity of travel agencies. Therefore, this law will include mechanisms that counter the loss of customers going for an organized vacation. The dynamics of changes in Europe and in the world make more and more directions dangerous, and therefore the market situation and the very functioning of travel agencies are a real risk of insolvency. The introduction of the Tourist Guarantee Fund will ensure that the interests of consumers in the event of geopolitical change are adequately secured. An important argument to support the further process of the law is that it was consulted very widely with stakeholders. 24 entities submitted their views, of which 7 did not make any comments and supported the essential assumptions of the draft law. It should be noted that many comments submitted, including from the marshals of individual voivodships, were included in the project. According to the amendment of the Act, the guarantor of the payment of funds and the person entitled to act in favour of customers in matters of payment of travel contracts is still the Marshal of the voivodship. The provisions of the Act also essentially regulate the scope of the data to be included in the list of tourist agreements. So far, tourism companies have had more flexibility in this area, which has not protected sufficiently the use of tourist services. Greater rights for tourists will also be translated into more possibilities of control of tourism organisers and tourist intermediaries, to which the Marshals of voivodships and the Minister responsible for tourism are entitled. From the point of view of consumers, it also seems important to clarify the concept of the cost of returning customers from a tourist event. According to the amendment, it will cover both transport costs and necessary transfers, accommodation, including those incurred individually by customers. In turn, an important law for tourism organisers and tourist intermediaries will be that of a differentiated contribution to the Tourist Guarantee Fund, which is linked to the level of risk of tourist events organised by them. Amenities for entrepreneurs will also be the possibility to draw up a declaration on the agreements concluded for a tourist event or tourism mediation by means of electronic communication. The Act introduces a number of important changes that will help to safeguard the interests of consumers of tourist services. The Free and Solidarni Circle fully supports the contested bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

These short-term effects are due to the uncertainty of investors who are concerned about the future of the British economy, but, as I said earlier, the government is prepared and monitoring this situation on an ongoing basis. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u475	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! There were very many questions about a very crucial issue in the negotiations, which we have before us, namely the rights of Poles who live in very large numbers, who learn, live in the UK, and who today are actually concerned about the shape of protecting their rights, what their legal situation will be during the process of leaving the UK from the European Union and, above all, when Britain's membership of the European Union will expire. This is not the only one, but one of the central elements of the mandate that we are creating in the government, the analysis that we are conducting in the government as regards the Polish attitude towards these two processes that are ahead of us, processes that will be fully dependent on Poland's position, the process of the UK's exit from the Union and the formation of new relations, the substitute relations between the European Union and the United Kingdom. As far as students specifically concerned, which Mrs Lichocka pointed out, today, of course, this system, which is used by many Poles, is open on an EU-wide basis. We do not expect the United Kingdom, together with its exit from the European Union, to close its higher education system, its research, development and scientific policies on Europe, because this would bring a clear loss to the university sector in the UK. On the other hand, EU rules, which force citizens of the Member States of the European Union to be treated equally, can be corrected. Surely there will be attempts to make such an adjustment. From our point of view, we will apply the same principle as in any other matter concerning rights and obligations. The principle of symmetry applies. If the United Kingdom really wants to remain an important participant in the common market, it must consider that it will also have to fulfil certain obligations that operate in the common market. We will not allow the United Kingdom to unilaterally lead to a situation where the benefits of the common market can be reaped and commitments are not met. Besides, these models are already in place. The United Kingdom will not be the only country with close relations with the common market. Ultimately, it will depend on itself and it is its sovereign decision how much it wants to pay for its separation with the European Union. But from our point of view, one thing is important: full symmetry of rights and obligations, full observation, very thorough observation of the legal status of each professional group, every citizen of a Member State of the European Union, especially Poland, in every situation of life. This is one of the central elements of our preparation for the two negotiation processes that are ahead of us. Several questions concerned the role of the UK in the future European architecture, the future architecture of international relations. Many of you referred to the fundamental foundation of the foreign minister's exposé, in which he pointed out Great Britain as a very important strategic partner of Poland in Europe and in the world. It seems that attention needs to be paid, especially in a debate such as today, that the European Union is not the only format that allows European countries to work together, intensive cooperation between European countries to benefit citizens in terms of prosperity and security. The most important platform for our cooperation, as demonstrated by the last summit, is NATO. The position of the UK, a member of the Security Council, a country that has one of the largest armies in Europe, a country that has military capacity, a country that has certain views on the geopolitical agenda of Europe, this position, regardless of membership of the European Union, will not change, so your doubts about the future status of the UK in our own geometry, security architecture, are completely unfounded. Regional consolidation, which is indeed an important element of the new government's policy, is not, contrary to your concerns, aimed at making life difficult for someone in Europe, one or the other capital. It is not aimed at turning the natural layout of the map of the European Union, NATO, Europe's map into a narrow map. Regional consolidation is designed to strengthen the region's voice on the same map that we have always been dealing with. In short, Visegrad consolidation, consolidation of the whole of Central Europe is not to suddenly change cooperation with Germany, France, the rest of the European Union to Central Europe, but to strengthen their status vis-à-vis the largest Member States of the European Union. The measure of the seriousness of the Polish government in preparing for two important negotiation processes is the fact that this mandate is already very detailed today. In this mandate, among those issues which are crucial for the rights of Poles, Polish citizens, the rights of entrepreneurs, and finally for the international position of Poland after the exit of Great Britain from the European Union, there are many issues. I'll pay attention to a few. The first issue is of course social, social, economic, human rights of all those Poles who live, learn and live in the UK. The second point is, of course, access to the market, to the market of services on which we have an advantage, a positive trade balance, and above all to the market of goods. In terms of agri-food goods and other goods, we have a commercial advantage that we want to preserve. Therefore, in the field of interest of the government conducting these negotiations, which we are talking about today, this issue will play no less important role. Important questions concerned the future of the common foreign and defence policy. It's true. The common foreign and defence policy of the European Union without Britain must certainly look different. But there is no need for the United Kingdom not to maintain its relationship with this policy, which is based on voluntary cooperation, on the willingness of individual Member States to cooperate. We will be in favour of a common foreign and defence policy of the European Union being open to countries such as the United Kingdom, which are added value in defence and in European policy, to those countries which are able to add something to the defence capacity of Europe abroad. There is no reason for this policy to be closed. There is certainly no reason for this policy to move towards non-complementary, confrontation with what is NATO's acquis. I have already mentioned research policy, this is a very important aspect that we will be paying attention to. I mentioned that we are clearly seeing problems in meeting the objectives of the UK's climate policy. We see the problems that can arise in agricultural policy when the United Kingdom, coming out of the common agricultural policy, will create national instruments to support its agricultural goods. All these elements are at the heart of the government's interest, they will shape our final position towards these negotiations, and all these things will have to be addressed correctly. Without this there will be no Polish agreement to restore the proper relationship between the European Union and Great Britain after leaving. And that's pretty specific. You've asked a lot of questions about specifics. I think this particular one is very clear. You really have to close your eyes and ears so you don't notice. Poland has very well, effectively moved its expectations on the principles of Britain's exit from the European Union. We have made it clear, together with the 26 countries of the European Union, that as long as the United Kingdom's membership of the European Union continues, the United Kingdom must fulfil all its treaty obligations, including the obligation to finance EU policy. This is clearly enshrined in the post- Summit position of the EU-27. As regards future relations, we have reiterated with other Member States that these relations should be as close as possible. We want the United Kingdom to be as closely connected as possible, as it is today, despite the lack of economic membership, politics and European defence. But the principle of symmetry that I was talking about at the beginning must be respected here. We are not inclined to take note of such an unsustainable agreement that would entail access to the common market, access to rights in Europe without respecting the obligations and expectations of the Member States, including Poland's expectations. We have a wide mandate for these negotiations. And the most important thing that was mentioned here was the reform of the European Union. Poland is certainly not a country that will allow European politicians to wake up in a few months to believe that nothing happened, that everything is fine with us, that in fact in the UK we have been dealing with an accident at work, by some misunderstanding caused by populists and people who did not understand something. That would be the worst lesson we can imagine. [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] I'd agree that...

She guarantees the consistency of the system of recording documents issued in succession cases, which significantly strengthens the credibility of the register of successions and increases the security of legal trade, and that is what I think, in particular, should depend on. The succession register shall be a public register to be kept by the National Notarial Council. It will disclose information on judgments and documents issued by notaries confirming the rights of inheritance. The European Certificate of Succession is an alternative document issued against the court's orders to establish the acquisition of an inheritance or notarial certificates of succession. In addition, it may be used by the recipient, the executor of the will or the estate manager. It shall be issued in cases where a legal title is required to be shown abroad. Its possession makes it easier to handle such matters. Since a court and notary are entitled to issue European Certificate of Succession in Poland, each of these authorities will have to disclose information on the European Certificate of Succession in the succession register as soon as possible in accordance with the draft amendment. A court or notary will charge a fee for entering an entry in the register of succession to be transferred to the National Council of Notaries. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will support this solution and support the amendment of the Code of Civil Procedure. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u493	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The Committee on Infrastructure adopted 17 editorial and legislative amendments and nine substantive amendments during the revision of the Aviation Law. Amendment 1. Substantively, the amendment to the construction of the article. 17. This is a provision that talks about the scope of the Aviation Law Act. As part of the revision of this article, we have deleted our mouths. 15 and 16, which had previously functioned in it, but their presence here meant that a member of the committee could not act as a judicial expert in matters conducted by the committee and that the members of the committee, when drawing up reports, analyses and opinions as experts, were to follow the principle of free assessment of evidence and were not bound by instructions as to the content of the resolutions taken. It was a mistake that we removed during this amendment. At the same time, such a smoothing change was the deletion of the paragraph. Article 1 82. On the other hand, the mouth has been added. 22 (2) in Article. 14. In the next amendment, Amendment 2, we made such a change that we replaced the words of a member of the committee with the word "person." In accordance with the provision in the previous wording, the chairman of the committee could not be a member of the committee unless he had at least 3 years of experience in safety investigations. In changing these words, we have led to the fact that the chairman of the committee may be a person with at least 3 years of experience in safety investigations. The situation is similar in the case of the mouth. 9 - the deputy chairman of the committee may be a person with at least 2 years experience in safety investigations. Amendment 3. We have made a change, which is that on the date of appointment with a member of the committee the employment relationship has been established, while the provision so far stated that on the date of appointment of a member of the committee there is a contract of employment with that person. The amendment eliminates the erroneous wording while replacing it with a new one. In Amendment 4, we have made changes to the article. 1 and in the mouth. 17 We have clarified that the members of the committee are not bound in the formulation of reports by anybody's instructions. And in the mouth. 22 We have also made a change which makes it clear that the qualification of safety incidents in order to take a decision on the conduct or supervision of an aviation accident investigation may belong solely to the chairman or his appointed alternate, secretary or other member of the committee. The oversight of an aviation accident investigation shall not be carried out by a person who is not a member of the committee. With Amendment 5, we washed our mouths. 11, since the comprehensive rules governing the issue of on-call time for committee members are detailed in Article. 17a paragraph. 7. In Amendment 6, however, we set out the conditions for admission to public use of airports. The amendment was introduced in Article. 1 in point 3 in point (a) (b) where we have determined that the aerodrome of exclusive use may be opened to all aircraft within the time limits and hours fixed by the airport manager and made public. Following the registration of aerodromes, the change in accessibility for airport users becomes an aerodrome of public use not subject to certification, unless it is subject to the requirements of Regulation No 216/208 of the European Community. As part of Amendment 7, after point 4, we added point 4a, which specifies how to proceed when an airport is to be set up in a place where an aircraft already operates. This amendment sets out the scope of the documents and also specifies the authority responsible for issuing environmental decisions which will determine whether environmental conditions will be considered or whether the decision will be taken on the basis of the position of the President of the Civil Aviation Authority. Amendment 8 has been amended to specify that authorisations previously issued under existing legislation will expire on the date of entry into force of this Law. In the 9th amendment, we noted that the existing provisions apply to cases initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of this Act. The regulations contained in this draft law amending the Aviation Law Act introduce changes in the organisation of the State Aviation Accident Investigation Commission. Experience in this area so far indicates the need for changes to the existing rules underlying its functioning. The amendments made concern in particular the possibility of opening airports exclusively for public use and shortening the duration of the conversion procedure. In addition, the provisions of this law simplify the procedure leading to the making available of exclusive use to airports and greater access to this airport to the interested parties. The Members of the Committee on Infrastructure have accepted the proposed amendments. On behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure, I ask the High House to adopt these amendments and this Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u495	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the draft parliamentary law amending the Aviation Law Act. The aim of this bill is to improve the functioning and strengthen the guarantee of the independence of the State Air Accident Investigation Commission and to ensure the effective opening of airports of exclusive use for public use by clarifying the rules and creating realistic conditions for changing the availability of an exclusive airport without reducing the level of safety in air traffic. New edition of the article. The 17th Act, which regulates the functioning of the State Commission for the Investigation of Aviation Accidents, is due to the need to clarify the applicable rules and to ensure that they comply with European Union law. The proposed amendment also concerns the replacement of the existing model of appointment of committee members for their appointment and dismissal. According to the evaluation of the committee's work so far, this model has not worked. A solution to significantly affect the quality of functioning of committees may be the introduction of provisions similar to those adopted with regard to the State Commission for Railway Accidents Investigation. Experience shows that the regulations applied in the cited law are much better in practice. The project strengthens the role and independence of committees also by ensuring appropriate mechanisms and guaranteeing independence in the course of an aviation accident investigation, it shall ensure that its members and the experts involved are entitled to express their views freely within the framework of the expert studies, studies, opinions, analyses and reports drawn up. The second important issue concerning the matter of the draft amendment is the simplification of procedures leading to the making available to airports of exclusive use. This procedure will ensure greater availability of airports for those concerned by introducing a category of non-certified public service aerodromes that will replace an ineffective institution for the temporary opening of an aerodrome of exclusive use for public use. The increase in the number of airports that can accept aircraft on a similar basis to public airports will have a positive impact on competition in the air services market and will increase the interest of airlines. The procedure is to be simplified and accelerated and its limitations and requirements result should only be of the necessity to maintain air safety. Given the need to improve the functioning of the State Air Accident Investigation Commission, to increase its independence, as well as its willingness to support aviation in Poland, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the proposed changes. Mr. Marshal, based on the article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Rules of Procedure of the Republic of Poland Sejm I propose an amendment to the bill amending the Law on Aviation Law. This centralisation must cover the organisational units set up by the local government, by which budgetary units, budgetary establishments and municipal offices, district old offices and marshal offices must be understood, with the provision of adequate time for the local authorities to adjust the accounts. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u539	Mr. Marshal! Minister! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I was honoured to present an opinion on the draft bill on the rules of settlement for goods and services tax and to reimburse public funds for projects financed by the budget of the European Union or by the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement by local government units, print No 709. We have a revolutionary law, we have a very big challenge for local governments. We are undertaking to straighten out something that is not the fault of local governments, and, in order not to say so, even the rule of law by government and parliament, which has undoubtedly caused a bit of confusion among local governments themselves, who today have to deal with it. But for that to be good, it has to be accurate. Today, as a Member speaking on behalf of the club, I would like to draw attention to a detail that turns a lot of things around, i.e. as defined in Article. Point 2 (1) (a) Oh, it's about the definition of an organizational unit. At the moment, in the present version presented to us by the committee, we read: Whenever the law refers to an organizational unit, it means that the local government unit of the government or the local government institution of the budget is set up. Now such a remark - the consequence of the current entry is the exclusion from the centralization of VAT settlements in the district government of budgetary units such as: district labour offices, district building supervision inspectors, county fire department commands, social assistance houses, children's homes. They conduct or may sell subject to VAT, but these entities have not been set up by local authorities. This is an important element, which, ladies and gentlemen, will be dragonflies in further work. As we know, e.g. schools are created by the local government. Such an approach does not comply with the provisions of Directive 2006/112/EC on the basis of the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 29 September 2015 in Case C 276/14 of the municipality of Wrocław v. Minister for Finance, as this will lead to the situation that outside the centralisation will remain: (a) budgetary units - separate VAT taxable persons - carrying out taxable sales; (b) the taxable entity benefiting from the exemption due to low turnover; (c) budgetary units potentially capable of carrying out taxable sales. None of these entities meets the independence criteria which have been raised and analysed in the aforementioned. judgment. It is therefore impossible for these entities to account for themselves as independent taxable persons for VAT, while there are no other entities that could include in their VAT accounts the economic transactions of those entities. It is therefore necessary to change the definition of an organisational unit by removing from the current definition the reservation that it is an entity created - i.e. we are talking about cutting out the creation - by the local government. The definition should therefore be: Whenever the law refers to an organisational unit, we mean a local government budgetary unit or a local government budgetary establishment. As a result of the adoption of such a definition of centralisation, all budgetary units and budgetary establishments, both those set up by the local government and those created by law, will be subject to such a definition of centralisation. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the Kukiz club15 pursuant to Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm on the hands of the Marshal I make this amendment and hope that the High House will accept it. This may be a small detail, but - once again - as a result, it cleans it up and then avoids unnecessary mess. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
