ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u547	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my views on the report of the committee on the Government's draft bill on the rules of settlement in the tax on goods and services and on the reimbursement of public funds for projects financed by the budget of the European Union or by the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement by local government units, print Nos 709 and 748. The need to implement this regulation is the consequence of the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union in the case of the municipality of Wrocław v Minister of Finance concerning value added tax. It follows from the judgment that Directive 2006/112/EC must be interpreted as meaning that bodies governed by public law, such as municipal budgetary units which are the subject of the main proceedings, cannot be regarded as taxable persons of value added tax because they do not meet the criterion of independence laid down in that provision. Those entities shall not bear or be liable for any economic risk associated with the economic activity entrusted to them to carry out on behalf of and on behalf of the municipality or for any damage caused by that activity. In addition, the question of VAT was settled by the Supreme Administrative Court, which, in the composition of seven judges in connection with the decision of the NSA of 17 March 2015, adopted a resolution on 26 October 2015. In the light of this resolution, it should be considered that budgetary establishments, such as the budgetary units of local government units, do not have a tax separation under the provisions on goods and services tax. The consequence of the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union and the resolution of the Supreme Administrative Court is to centralise the accounts of the units established by the local government in the field of VAT in the municipalities, district old offices and marshal offices. What is the purpose of the bill? It provides local authorities with legal instruments both to support the process of centralisation in formal terms and to minimise the burden associated with it. The draft law provides for the succession of local government units which, as of the date on which VAT is cleared together with all organisational units, no later than 1 January 2017, will enter all rights and obligations in so far as they relate to the tax of their business units, including those which have not ceased to be an isolated VAT taxable person. This provision will ensure the succession of rights and obligations and will apply mutatis mutandis to settlements for periods prior to the date on which the tax is settled. While problems may arise when local government units accept the proportion of the tax calculation for each business unit, including for the office serving the local government unit operating in the form of a local government unit, in the case of carrying out tax-tax activities and tax-free activities with the help of that business unit, it is to be hoped that local authorities in practical operation will develop appropriate methods of management accounting to overcome these difficulties. In this situation, it is incomprehensible, and it should actually be regarded as a great lack of this legislation, that the position of local authorities on the draft law has not been obtained. There is no position of local government corporations or individual municipalities, both the Polish County Association and the voivodships. During the work in the Committee on Public Finance, amendments were made, mainly of a technical nature, by the Legislative Office of the Sejm. They make the law more coherent and more friendly in applying to taxpayers from local self-government units. Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle will vote in favour of the bill submitted to the High House. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

As far as costs are concerned, in 2016 projectors expect to increase expenditure on pensions and reduce the revenue from contributions of PLN 1.7 billion, but already in 2017 expenditure is expected to amount to PLN 3.2 billion. The costs are high and these estimates have been made with an additional assumption that only one out of every four eligible persons will benefit from the possibility of early retirement. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u621	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! (PL) Mr President, I would like to ask you a few questions. First of all, I would like to point out that there is no official position of the Council of Ministers regarding the citizens' draft law on the revision of the Social Insurance Fund pension law that has been discussed today, but it can be said that we are dealing with a new situation that you mentioned in the discussion today. This new situation is that since November 2015 in the Sejm there has been a presidential bill amending the Social Insurance Fund Pension Act, which would reduce the statutory retirement age or restore it to the state before 1 January 2013, i.e. to 60 years for women and 65 years for men. Secondly, since Tuesday we also have a positive position of the Council of Ministers vis-à-vis the presidential draft law reducing the retirement age. One can say this: what connects both positions, the position of the Council of Ministers and the position of applicants is that we have a need to make the process of retirement more flexible. Namely, the solutions that were introduced in May 2012, increasing the retirement age - I think we will agree - are too strict, too rigid solutions. There is a need to make the retirement process more flexible. On the other hand, when it comes to instruments, how to achieve this goal, it can be said that we differ in this. The Presidential draft law reducing retirement age assumes a reduction in the statutory retirement age without linking it to the condition of internship. Today's bill assumes that this flexibility would consist in using only the basis of the traineeship, and this internship understood as a contribution period. This issue was discussed when the PSL Parliamentary Club submitted a twin bill amending the Social Insurance Fund pension law. Then, too, this argument from my mouth fell or was presented. Namely, the fundamental question arises as to whether the introduction of such a solution, which gives the right to a pension only upon fulfilment of the conditions relating to a traineeship, is in accordance with Article. 67 of the Constitution, which, as it was said today, was invoked during the discussion, says that citizens have the right to social security, but in the event of achievement or after reaching a certain retirement age. I would like to say that, against the background of these differences in the approach to making the transition more flexible, one can also see, as far as correlation is concerned, a lack of correlation, i.e. a lack of correlation between these solutions. Namely, the citizens' project, which is being discussed today, assumes the introduction of an internship pension, a pension dependent on the grounds of service, but assuming that the retirement age introduced in 2012 increased to 67 years old will be maintained. It can be said that, in the event of the implementation or adoption of a presidential bill reducing the retirement age, there will be no correlation between the two solutions. This lack of correlation will consist primarily in the fact that reducing the retirement age of women to 60 years of age will result in only a few ladies having a chance to benefit from this solution. The question therefore arises as to whether it is appropriate to lay a plea for unequal treatment on grounds of sex and - it can be said - discrimination against women. Shouldn't it be appropriate, given the solutions contained in the presidential draft law reducing retirement age, to propose e.g. diversification of the pension period for women and men? There was a question about partial pensions. I agree with the fact that partial pensions were introduced under the law or when a law was passed increasing the retirement age. To be honest, from the very beginning, I have been critical of this solution and this solution still deserves such a critical assessment. First of all, this solution, when presented, was intended to be a cover function, i.e. was intended to apply to those who, before reaching this increased retirement age, but after reaching a certain lower age threshold: 62 years old - women, 65 years old - men, already had a longer life insurance period of 35 years for women and 40 years for men. It can be said that the critical assessment of this solution was that partial pensions were supposed to be very low and that this was supposed to be used by people in difficulty in the labour market who were losing their jobs before the retirement age. It can be said that partial pensions were supposed to amount to half of the final pension that would have been granted once this basic retirement age had been reached. This assumption turned out to be completely wrong. Namely, it turned out that partial pensions are an attractive service for people who achieved high incomes, high salaries - this is evidenced by the average amount of currently paid partial pensions, which amounts to about 1800 PLN - for persons who legitimized themselves or legitimized themselves for a relatively long period of insurance. These people just, not wanting to wait until retirement age... [[Good.]] ...have decided to take advantage of the possibility of collecting part-time pensions, but with the possibility of simultaneously combining this benefit with work. [[It's a law.]] So it can be said that the recipient to whom these benefits were to be addressed was simply unheard of. I would like to conclude that these solutions have been - we talked about this in November, I remember the discussion - assessed by the Constitutional Court, which considered that the differentiation of the conditions for the acquisition of the right to a partial pension, and in the area of the age of partial pension entitlement, and in the area of traineeship, where a solution is introduced which aims to harmonise the basic retirement age, is simply incompatible with the Constitution, infringes the principle of equal treatment. So for partial pensions, You can really make a lot of accusations. And maybe one more comment on the draft law on the amendment of the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund, which we are discussing. On the other hand, it is difficult to treat this solution in terms of an attractive solution for the next generation of insured people - insured people working in completely different realities in the labour market, in the realities about which Mrs Tomaszewska, among others, is responsible. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u653	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, a position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Senate's resolution on the Constitutional Tribunal Act. The Constitutional Tribunal Act adopted by the Sejm on 7 July resolves some of the important problems concerning the functioning of the Tribunal. We believe that the amendments tabled by the Senate and the committee's positive opinions are compromises. The Senate has made several substantive changes to the Act and several legislative amendments. The substantive amendments meet the expectations of the opposition and legal environments, including the Constitutional Tribunal. It is worth noting that we limit the principle of dealing with cases according to the order of influence. Where the rights and freedoms of the individual are concerned, as well as public order and security, the President of the Tribunal will be able to change the order in which cases are dealt with. We allow the Ombudsman to all proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. We waive the President's involvement in disciplinary proceedings against the judges of the tribunal. We clarify the provision on the jurisdiction of the Court to examine normative acts, as well as the regulation on the presentation by the General Assembly of candidates for President of the Tribunal to the President. We welcome the fact that the Act in its current form has received support in the Senate that goes beyond our political environment. With reference to earlier speeches in the High House, I will repeat that in matters that arouse a lot of political emotions and wake them up for a long time, a destructive approach is a principle: everything or nothing. Since the amendments tabled are another step towards limiting the area of the dispute around the Constitutional Tribunal, acting on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club supports the adoption of 26 of the 27 amendments adopted by the Senate. Like the Committee on Justice, we are only against the technical amendment of paragraph 25. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u799	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! August 14th this year We will celebrate the 75th anniversary of the death of Maximilian Maria Kolbe - the Polish Franciscan, the Presbyter, the missionary and the Holy Catholic Church, the first Polish martyr during World War II. Maximilian Maria Kolbe, or Rajmund Kolbe, was born on January 8, 1894, in Zduńska Wola. His mother was Polish, and his father was German. The difficult financial situation forced Kolby's parents to move. Initially they lived in Łódź, and later in Pabianice, where they both found work. In June 1902, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) Kolbe accepted the first Holy Communion. In the church of St. Matthew in Pabianice had a vision. Our Lady appeared to him then. So he decided to follow his calling and, at the request of the Franciscan missionaries, with the permission of his parents, joined the Franciscan order. At the age of 13, he began his studies at a small seminar in Lviv. After 3 years he began the novitiate in the Order, taking the name Maximilian. In 1912. Kolbe started his studies in Krakow. After a few months, he was sent to Rome, the International Seraphite College. In 1914, he made everlasting vows and took Mary's name. Then he got two PhDs from philosophy and theology. 28 April 1918 Maximilian Maria Kolbe accepted priestly ordination. The Holy Mass was celebrated in the church of Sant'Andrea delle Fratte in Rome. The following year he returned to Poland and took lectures. During his stay in Rome, he and his fellow believers established a relationship between the devout Knights of the Immaculate One. In order to keep in touch with its members, from 1922 he published a monthly magazine in Krakow, the Immaculate Knight, who several years later reached a million copies. In time, he began to publish the Little Journal. The letters included religious, social, political and cultural subjects. In 1927. Kolbe founded the Immaculate Monastery under Warsaw. Between 1931 and 1935, he served as a missionary in Japan, where he founded the Immaculate Japanese. It also opened similar centres in China and India. At the age of 42, he returned to Poland to guide the Immaculate, which in the meantime became the largest Catholic monastery in the world. His activity was suspended during the German occupation. Kolbe was arrested that day. After his release, he organized a service centre for the surrounding population. After he was arrested again, he was imprisoned in Pawiak. 28 May 1941 Kolbe was sent to a concentration camp in Auschwitz. A few months later, he voluntarily chose to die a starving death, saving her convicted fellow prisoner Franciszek Gajowniczek, who, after moving to another camp, was liberated. Maximilian Kolbe died on 14 August 1941 in the death block. The camp officer, the head of the Hans Bock infirmary, killed him with a shot of poison. Kolby's father's body was burned in the camp crematorium. The efforts in the Holy See to raise Father Maximilian to the altars were made after the end of World War II. At the beginning of 1969 Pope Paul VI announced a decree on the heroicity of virtues o. Maximilian. Two years later, a decree was issued stating the miraculous healings of Father Kolbe's intercession. He was beatified as a martyr on October 17, 1971. On October 10, 1982, he was canonized by Pope John Paul. II. In view of the 45th anniversary of the beatification of the monk, the 75th anniversary of his martyrdom and the 100th anniversary of the Association of the Immaculate Knights, the solemn opening of the exhibition dedicated to St. Father Maximilian Kolbe. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u800	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! At present, collective passenger transport is based primarily on the principles of free competition of transport companies, limited and secured by the obligation to obtain authorisation to provide such services in the area concerned. Aware of the need to implement EU legislation, it should be noted that the introduction of such a collective transport system will lead to a monopolism of the market and exclusion of small transport operators who do not have the possibility (e.g. a sufficiently large rolling stock) to compete with larger operators. According to the provisions of the Collective Transport Act, yes, companies with which the organiser will not sign the contract will actually be able to provide transport services, but because they will not receive compensation that operators will receive, their survival on the market will be put into question. The compensation for the reductions applied constitutes a significant part of the revenue for such carriers. On the other hand, there is a danger for passengers entitled to relief (students, students, pensioners, pensioners, etc.) who will have to pay for the full amount of the ticket on lines that will not be classified as utility lines. Self-governments do not hesitate to introduce a public collective transport system due to a number of uncertainties in the provisions of the Public Collective Transport Act. They shall relate in particular to: 1) the problem of the responsibility of the local government unit for not obtaining the so-called operator. reasonable profit, 2) The problem of exclusive use of bus stops as so-called. operator stops, 3) the problem of how to set ticket prices on public utility lines. It is important to appreciate the legislative initiative of the Senators' Group to extend the vacathio legis of the Public Collective Transport Act to be necessary to maintain stability in this area for a year. However, this period seems to be too short. In fact, the law in this form is a radical exclusion of the most deprived passengers from many small towns from collective transport covered by reliefs, it is a monopolisation of the market for small carriers, and therefore a serious impediment to the functioning of many Polish small companies. Finally, the Act in its current form creates legal chaos for local governments, additional expenditure of a large scale, the need for additional employment. There is therefore a need for a thorough overhaul of the law or to consider remaining in the current system. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-21-sejm-23-3.u801	On 20 July 1797 the first public performance of Mazurka Dąbrowski took place in the Italian Reggio Emilia. The anthem was originally called the Song of Polish Legions in Italy. The author of the melody is still unknown, but it is based on folk mazora. The words were arranged between July 16 and 19 by Józef Wybicki in the Italian Reggio nell'Emilia. He was a well-deserved patriot, creating invaluable foundations for the formation of the Legions of the Polish Eaters of the Lombardy Republic. The text of the song was first disseminated in Mantua in February 1799 by printing in the newspaper.Dekada Legionowa, thanks to which soldiers met it, and then brought it on their lips, returning from wandering to the country, making the mazurka very popular and soon performed on all significant occasions. It was created as a protest against erase Poland from the map of Europe. The words of the Polish Legions in Italy referred to the lofty events of the history of Polish weapons and modern military leaders, in which they hoped to return to their homeland and regain independence. From the very beginning the song was accepted with applause by Legion Dąbrowski. At the beginning of 1798 it was also known in the country in all partitions. It was sung during the November Uprising (1830), January (1863), by Poles in the Great Emigration during the 1905, I and II World War Revolution. The mazurka text was translated by poets who solidarity with the fighting Polish and was known in 17 languages, among others. German, French, English, Russian, Hungarian, Croatian, Macedonian, Serbian, Slovak and Tzmudz. During the Spring of the Peoples (1848) Mazurek Dąbrowski was sung on the streets of Vienna, Berlin and Prague, where he was particularly popular. Mazurek Dąbrowski, Polish patriotic song, since 26 February 1927 the decision of the Polish Sejm became the official national anthem of the Republic of Poland. Mazurek Dąbrowski is 219 years old. It is still a living symbol of Polishness and the spirit of the nation. The anthem still remains one of the highest values valued by Poles. Thank you.
